THIRD - THE BACKGROUND. Every background detail stays in focus; the box remains dominant through its scale and placement.

Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few small pinecones, two or three small red and turquoise glass baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own carton.

THE WREATH - it hangs on the FACE of the door, centred on the upper half of the door and comfortably inside the frame with clear door surface visible all the way around it. It must NOT be cut off by the right edge of the picture and must NOT drift onto the door frame or the wall. The whole circle of the wreath and the whole red velvet bow are visible.

Do not crush the blacks and do not let any part of the frame go to a flat dark mass.

SHARPNESS - this frame must be tack sharp edge to edge: every moulding edge and panel corner in the door, the machined edge and the patina of the brass knob, the individual needles of the pine wreath and the woven nap of the red velvet bow, the skin texture and fine hairs on the forearm, the grain of the floorboards. BANNED: blur, soft focus, haze, mushy low-detail surfaces, waxy skin, a dreamy or filtered look, motion blur, noise, upscaling artefacts.

## C05 F2 - The door opens on the room — room reference
**Frame:** `references/v28_c05_r1.png`
**Settings:** gpt_image_2_5 · sunburst · 9:16 · 2k · medium · 1520×2688 · count 1 · media role image → image_references (backend)
**Job ID:** `cc93a56e-be13-48d7-8d1c-e57b7da4db59`
**References:**
- @Image 1: `content/video_11_assets/references/v11_c05_r1.png` · Higgsfield media `dda4701e-8d9d-4351-99f8-60bb9093cf68`
- @Image 2: `content/video_9_assets/references/v9_c05_r4.png` · Higgsfield media `1c500339-2855-45b6-bd9b-4c3633b5a272`
**Lineage:** Reused from v24 C05 r1 - changed: set nouns only (Santa Fe pueblo revival; hands, sweater, table/counter/kitchen, door, children, pyjamas, room, fold sentences, projection theme as applicable).

### Image prompt
````text
Recreate @Image 1's camera, framing, scale and poses. Wholesome family Christmas scene. @Image 1 owns composition geometry only. @Image 2 owns the dense saturated photographic projection look; change the architecture and children to the descriptions below. Both reference images are generated stills, not photographs of a documented real house.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same wide framing of a whole tall room seen from a doorway, the same natural low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself, and the same two children - a boy standing TURNED AROUND FACING THE CAMERA, front-on, his face fully visible and lit by the coloured light, looking towards the lens with a delighted open-mouthed smile, arms slightly out in wonder, and a girl beside him seen from behind, still looking up at the projected wall.

The boy is of about six with fair skin and short curly chestnut-brown hair; the girl is of about five with fair skin and long straight platinum-blonde hair in a single braid. Both children are fully dressed for winter in forest-green and white brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets printed with little white snowmen and red mittens, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.

CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height Santa Fe pueblo-revival Christmas great room with a high flat VIGA-AND-LATILLA CEILING: a row of massive round dark pine viga beams spans the room from side to side, with pale peeled latilla poles laid in a herringbone pattern across them. Smooth rounded white adobe plaster walls. A tall rounded white adobe chimney breast stands on the back wall with a thick pine beam mantel; a wall of tall deep-set windows with carved pine frames and muntins occupies the left wall and a wide carved pine doorway with a rounded adobe surround sits on the right. A saltillo terracotta tile floor, a large rust-red leather sectional sofa, a round woven-wool ottoman and a soft red-black-and-cream Southwestern-pattern wool rug. Seen from the doorway; the new a turquoise-painted carved Santa Fe pine door with four square panels, a satin finish and a thick rounded white adobe surround is open out of frame.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red, turquoise and gold glass baubles, tinsel and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-turquoise knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY CHRISTMAS HARBOUR TOWN: colourful gabled harbour houses with glowing windows and snowy roofs, a curl of white smoke rising from every chimney, little fishing boats strung with Christmas lights bobbing at the quay, a lighthouse sweeping its golden beam across the bay, families pulling sledges along the waterfront, and tall snow-laden fir trees on the hills behind. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the rooftops into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and harbour blue dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The flat latilla ceiling and the deep round vigas remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting the walls at crisp lines. The sleigh and the ceiling picture wrap over and under every viga, continuing straight across their round faces and across the herringbone latillas between them, before continuing onto the walls. The tall adobe chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The carved pine window frames and muntins, the deep-set openings and the carved pine doorway carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the harbour town, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The terracotta tile floor, the soft Southwestern-pattern wool rug, the rust-red leather sectional sofa, the round woven-wool ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and harbour blue dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The flat latilla ceiling and the deep round vigas remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting the walls at crisp lines. The sleigh and the ceiling picture wrap over and under every viga, continuing straight across their round faces and across the herringbone latillas between them, before continuing onto the walls. The tall adobe chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The carved pine window frames and muntins, the deep-set openings and the carved pine doorway carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET, AND THE PREVIOUS ATTEMPT FAILED IT: every exposed wooden beam, rafter, truss, purlin, window frame, mullion, muntin, glazing bar, door frame and casing is COVERED by the projection. Treat the projected image as one continuous sheet of coloured light that ignores the wood: where a beam crosses the ceiling, the stars, snowflakes, the sleigh, the reindeer and the golden trail continue straight over the beam's underside and both of its sides, cut into strips by its edges and shifted slightly by its depth, in the same full colour and brightness as the ceiling around it; where a window frame, mullion or door casing crosses the wall, the garlands, lanterns and scenery continue straight over its face in the same full colour as the wall beside it. Seen from the camera, the wooden members are camouflaged inside the picture - their shape reads only from thin shadow lines and a slight shift of the image along their edges, never from their own bare wood colour. NOT ONE beam, rafter, frame, mullion or casing appears as a plain bare wooden stripe, a dark silhouette or an unlit outline cutting through the picture. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside; the frames around the glass carry the picture. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The terracotta tile floor, the soft Southwestern-pattern wool rug, the rust-red leather sectional sofa, the round woven-wool ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but its colour sits a touch closer to real life - rich, not neon - and it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls: the plaster texture and wood grain show faintly through it, and the shadows keep a little more ordinary room detail, with the furniture, rug and floor clearly readable in the soft spill.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the grain of the pine vigas and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

@Image 3 is a wood-coverage example only: it shows the projection lying straight across exposed wooden trusses and braces. Copy only that coverage of the wood; do not copy its room, architecture, children, colours or projected scenes.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.

True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but its colour sits a touch closer to real life - rich, not neon - and it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls: the plaster texture and wood grain show faintly through it, and the shadows keep a little more ordinary room detail, with the furniture, rug and floor clearly readable in the soft spill.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the surface texture of the white adobe plaster and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

@Image 3 is a wood-coverage example only: it shows the projection lying straight across exposed wooden trusses and braces. Copy only that coverage of the wood; do not copy its room, architecture, children, colours or projected scenes.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
````

### Video prompt
````text
ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE - ABSOLUTE RULE: this 5-second clip is a single uninterrupted real-time shot from the start image to the last frame. No cut, no jump cut, no hidden edit, no time skip, no dissolve, no zoom, no reframe. Everything in it happens continuously, frame to frame.

PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP. The Christmas picture on the walls and the viga-and-latilla ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, like a TV screen showing an animated Christmas movie - never a still picture. Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in EVERY frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is never parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc from where it is first seen across the viga-and-latilla ceiling toward the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. The reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, NEVER SKIPPED: Every harbour house in the projected picture has a chimney on its roof, and from EVERY one of those chimneys a thick, bright white plume of smoke pours out continuously from the very first frame to the last - clearly visible against the dark night sky, never faint, never thin, never missing on any house. THE GIRL lands from the jump shown in the start image onto the sofa cushions and keeps bouncing along the sofa with small, springy, real-speed bounces - her feet leave the cushion by only 15 to 25 centimetres, about two bounces per second, the cushion visibly sinking on every landing - moving a little further along the sofa with each bounce while she points one arm straight up at Santa's flying sleigh, head tipped back, mouth wide open, squealing with delight.
THE BOY walks slowly backwards across the rug with his head tipped far back, one arm stretched up pointing at the sleigh, turning his body to follow it as it flies across the ceiling; around 2 seconds he glances across at his sister, points up again and laughs, then keeps stepping and gazing up.
Neither child ever stands still, poses or faces the camera. Real children moving at real speed with real weight: quick, snappy bounces under normal gravity, never slow motion, never floating, never a high exaggerated leap, no morphing limbs.

TIMELINE (camera):
0.0-1.0s the person filming takes one small step forward; the frame moves in slightly and settles.
1.0-2.0s the camera holds in the closer position.
2.0-4.0s the camera tilts up only a little, about 8 degrees, in one slow smooth move, so the vaulted latilla ceiling fills no more than the top third of the frame - it stays inside the projected picture the whole time and never reaches the unlit upper planks. The ENTIRE vaulted latilla ceiling in view, every plane, beam and corner, is covered with the projection - starry midnight sky, the silver full moon, the snowflakes and the flying sleigh - no bare plaster, no bare wood, no unprojected corner anywhere in view. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.
No zoom - the only forward movement is the one small physical step.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing, gasping and squealing, cushions thumping, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

SURFACE MAPPING - The two sloping latilla planes, the ridge viga and the rafter vigas remain stationary. The drawings fold at the ridge and run across the round faces of the ridge viga and the rafter vigas, fold around the adobe chimney corners and carry across the carved pine window frames, muntins and the carved pine doors, with correct surface perspective and occlusion. All physical architecture stays stationary.

DO NOT INCLUDE: bare, blank, white or partly unprojected ceiling, beam, corner or wall in any frame, especially at the end when the camera looks up; a camera that tilts up beyond the projected picture; a projection appearing, fading in or revealing itself; children standing still; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; a third child; an adult; any music; the projector unit in shot; a zoom; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

AT EVERY MOMENT AT LEAST TWELVE DIFFERENT DRAWN THINGS ARE VISIBLY MOVING AT THE SAME TIME, spread across the left wall, the back wall, the right wall and the ceiling, each one with big, bold, easy-to-read motion that a viewer notices on a phone screen at a glance, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else travels, turns, rises, flutters or bursts. EVERY ANIMAL, BIRD, PERSON AND VEHICLE IN THE PICTURE IS A FLAT HAND-DRAWN CARTOON MADE OF PROJECTED LIGHT lying on the wall or ceiling surface - never a real, photographic, furry or three-dimensional creature. Nothing ever leaves the wall, stands out from it or flies into the room; every drawn thing moves only inside the flat projected picture.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, CHECKED FIRST: look at the houses in the start image - the row of red, cream and green gabled harbour houses with snowy roofs and glowing windows along the LEFT wall, and the tall gabled townhouses along the RIGHT wall. Every one of those roofs carries a chimney pot, and from EACH chimney a thick, bright WHITE column of smoke is already pouring out in the very first frame: big round puffs push out of the chimney top one after another, swell, curl and sway, and climb up the wall about one roof-height every second, stretching into long wavy trails that drift toward the ceiling before fading. At every moment at least SIX separate, wide, bright white smoke columns are visibly rising - three or more over the left-wall houses and three or more over the right-wall houses - as obvious as steam from a boiling kettle against the dark night sky. A house without rising smoke makes the clip wrong.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling in one smooth sweeping arc, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so it is in a clearly different place every second - never parked with only its legs moving. The reindeer gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, Santa waves, and the long golden trail streams and curls behind it, shedding glittering sparks.
- THE SHIPS: the tall ships and fishing boats strung with Christmas lights in the harbour bob and rock on the waves, their lit sails swelling and little red flags fluttering, and one lit ship sails clearly across the harbour from right to left during the clip.
- THE LIGHTHOUSE: its golden beam sweeps round in a continuous circle about every two seconds, raking across the water, the houses and up over the ceiling.
- THE WATER: waves roll across the harbour and bright glints of light dance on them.
- THE PEOPLE: little families walk along the snowy quay pulling sledges, about one house-width every second; children throw snowballs; skaters glide on the frozen edge of the harbour.
- SHOOTING STARS AND FIREWORKS: a new shooting star streaks across the ceiling about every second, leaving a glittering tail; small round golden and red fireworks burst in the night sky above the harbour one after another, open into sparkles and fade.
- LIGHTS: the strings of golden lights along the upper walls twinkle in running chase patterns that race along the garlands; the red velvet bows sway; the stars twinkle and the silver moon glows.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift and swirl down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the walls and ceiling, never real snow in the room.
- THE HOUSES: the windows of the harbour houses flicker warmly on and off; wreaths on the doors sway.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: from the very first frame the sleigh is flying, the lighthouse beam is sweeping and six thick white smoke columns are rising from the house chimneys on both walls.
1-2s: the sleigh travels a quarter of the ceiling; a lit ship sails across the harbour behind the bed; a firework bursts; the smoke keeps climbing.
2-3s: the sleigh flies on; a shooting star streaks over; the lights chase along the garlands; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying; the boats rock; the families pull their sledges; two fireworks burst; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
4-5s: everything still moving at full pace in the last frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real adobe plaster, the vigas and latillas, the nicho and the carved pine window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, the bed or the boy.

CAMERA - ONE slow, gentle, continuous tilt DOWN from the first frame to the last, like a parent filming - the camera is NEVER static and the movement is clearly visible in every second.

0.0-5.0s: the phone tilts DOWN smoothly at an even pace by about 12 degrees in total: the projected ceiling slides up and partly out of the top of the frame while more of the bed, the covers, the bench and the rug come into view at the bottom. Because the camera only ever moves DOWN from the start framing, no ceiling area outside the start image is ever seen. The boy stays fully in frame from head to feet.
One continuous take: no cut, no zoom, no push-in, no pull-back, never a tilt up, never a locked-off static frame.

THE BOY BOUNCES LIKE A REAL CHILD ON A REAL MATTRESS - natural, believable and unexaggerated, at real speed for all five seconds. Small, springy, continuous bounces on the same spot in the middle of the bed: his feet leave the mattress by only about 10 to 20 centimetres, about two bounces per second, the soft mattress visibly sinking under his feet on every landing and pushing him back up. REAL-TIME SPEED, NOT SLOW MOTION: each bounce is quick and snappy, taking only about half a second from push-off to landing under normal gravity, exactly like a real child filmed at normal speed - the footage is never slowed down, never time-stretched and never dreamy. Knees soft, head tipped back, mouth open, laughing up at the ceiling in amazement. While he bounces he throws one arm straight up and points at Santa's sleigh, turning his head to follow it as it flies across the ceiling, then drops the arm and points again. His body keeps exactly the same size, proportions, face and clothing in every frame; he stays in the same spot on the bed. No high leaps, no hanging in the air, no floating, no hopping across the bed, no stiff puppet-like body, no morphing limbs.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, a child at home with his family at Christmas. Crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed, sharp from first frame to last.

ASSET ROLES: the start image is the approved still that controls a bright-proportioned Santa Fe pueblo-revival bedroom with a high SHED CEILING of dark round vigas and pale latillas sloping up from the front of the room to the tall back wall. A carved knotty-pine bed with a wide upholstered cream headboard is centred on the flat rounded white adobe back wall between two tall deep-set windows with carved pine frames and muntins, a carved pine nicho shelf above, white bedding with a red striped Chimayo wool blanket, pine bedside chests, a saltillo terracotta tile floor and a soft cream wool rug. Preserve the exact child identity, clothing, furniture, colours and the full projected artwork of the start image; its framing is the FIRST frame of this clip.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about six with fair skin and short curly chestnut-brown hair, fully dressed for winter in a long-sleeved forest-green and white brushed-cotton Christmas top printed with little white snowmen and red mittens, with matching loose lounge trousers and white wool socks, standing on the bed. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the walls and the whole sloping viga ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster and the wood is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY CHRISTMAS HARBOUR TOWN: colourful gabled harbour houses with glowing windows and snowy roofs, a curl of white smoke rising from every chimney, little fishing boats strung with Christmas lights bobbing at the quay, a lighthouse sweeping its golden beam across the bay, families pulling sledges along the waterfront, and tall snow-laden fir trees on the hills behind. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the rooftops into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and harbour blue dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing and gasping in delight. (no BGM). No subtitles.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a still, frozen or printed-looking projection; a projection that only twinkles; a sleigh parked in one place; chimneys without smoke; a house without rising smoke; boats that do not bob; a lighthouse beam that does not sweep; families that do not travel; snow that does not fall; bare, white or unprojected ceiling or wall; a camera that starts higher, wider or anywhere other than the start image; a static, locked-off camera; a tilt up; a pan, zoom, push-in or pull-back; any drawn animal or figure that looks real or leaves the wall; bare or unprojected ceiling in the first frame; any house or cottage without a thick rising smoke column; fewer than twelve things moving at once; a calm or slow projection; slow-motion or floaty jumping; high exaggerated leaps; the boy changing size or shape; real snow or particles in the air of the room; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The sloping viga ceiling remains clearly three-dimensional, meeting the walls at crisp lines. The sleigh and the ceiling picture are foreshortened along the slope, wrap straight across the round faces of the vigas and the latillas, and fold at the wall line before continuing down the walls. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. Treat the projected image as one continuous sheet of coloured light that ignores the wood: where a beam crosses the ceiling, the stars, snowflakes, the sleigh, the reindeer and the golden trail continue straight over the beam's underside and both of its sides, cut into strips by its edges and shifted slightly by its depth, in the same full colour and brightness as the ceiling around it; where a window frame, mullion or door casing crosses the wall, the garlands, lanterns and scenery continue straight over its face in the same full colour as the wall beside it. Seen from the camera, the wooden members are camouflaged inside the picture - their shape reads only from thin shadow lines and a slight shift of the image along their edges, never from their own bare wood colour. NOT ONE beam, rafter, frame, mullion or casing appears as a plain bare wooden stripe, a dark silhouette or an unlit outline cutting through the picture. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside; the frames around the glass carry the picture. There is NO falloff anywhere. EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

The terracotta tile floor, the soft cream wool rug, the bed and its covers, the bench at its foot and the little Christmas tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP, AND IT MUST BE BUSY AND ALIVE. The Christmas picture on the walls and the sloping viga ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, like a TV screen showing a lively animated Christmas movie - never a still picture, never a calm picture. Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. AT EVERY MOMENT AT LEAST TWELVE DIFFERENT DRAWN THINGS ARE VISIBLY MOVING AT THE SAME TIME, spread across the left wall, the back wall, the right wall and the ceiling, each one with big, bold, easy-to-read motion that a viewer notices on a phone screen at a glance, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else travels, turns, rises, flutters or bursts. EVERY ANIMAL, BIRD, PERSON AND VEHICLE IN THE PICTURE IS A FLAT HAND-DRAWN CARTOON MADE OF PROJECTED LIGHT lying on the wall or ceiling surface - never a real, photographic, furry or three-dimensional creature. Nothing ever leaves the wall, stands out from it or flies into the room; every drawn thing moves only inside the flat projected picture.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, CHECKED FIRST: look at the houses in the start image - the row of red, cream and green gabled harbour houses with snowy roofs and glowing windows along the LEFT wall, and the tall gabled townhouses along the RIGHT wall. Every one of those roofs carries a chimney pot, and from EACH chimney a thick, bright WHITE column of smoke is already pouring out in the very first frame: big round puffs push out of the chimney top one after another, swell, curl and sway, and climb up the wall about one roof-height every second, stretching into long wavy trails that drift toward the ceiling before fading. At every moment at least SIX separate, wide, bright white smoke columns are visibly rising - three or more over the left-wall houses and three or more over the right-wall houses - as obvious as steam from a boiling kettle against the dark night sky. A house without rising smoke makes the clip wrong.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling in one smooth sweeping arc, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so it is in a clearly different place every second - never parked with only its legs moving. The reindeer gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, Santa waves, and the long golden trail streams and curls behind it, shedding glittering sparks.
- THE SHIPS: the tall ships and fishing boats strung with Christmas lights in the harbour bob and rock on the waves, their lit sails swelling and little red flags fluttering, and one lit ship sails clearly across the harbour from right to left during the clip.
- THE LIGHTHOUSE: its golden beam sweeps round in a continuous circle about every two seconds, raking across the water, the houses and up over the ceiling.
- THE WATER: waves roll across the harbour and bright glints of light dance on them.
- THE PEOPLE: little families walk along the snowy quay pulling sledges, about one house-width every second; children throw snowballs; skaters glide on the frozen edge of the harbour.
- SHOOTING STARS AND FIREWORKS: a new shooting star streaks across the ceiling about every second, leaving a glittering tail; small round golden and red fireworks burst in the night sky above the harbour one after another, open into sparkles and fade.
- LIGHTS: the strings of golden lights along the upper walls twinkle in running chase patterns that race along the garlands; the red velvet bows sway; the stars twinkle and the silver moon glows.
- DRAWN SNOW, FLAT ON THE WALLS ONLY: small flat cartoon snowflakes, drawn like the rest of the picture, slide slowly down the painted night sky on the walls and the ceiling, lying flat on the surface and bending round the corners with the picture - part of the projected drawing, like snow painted on wallpaper, never coming off the wall. THE AIR OF THE ROOM STAYS COMPLETELY CLEAR: no snowflakes, sparkles, glitter, dust or particles float or fall anywhere in the room - not in front of the boy, the bed, the Christmas tree or the camera, and not in the space between the camera and the walls.
- THE HOUSES: the windows of the harbour houses flicker warmly on and off; wreaths on the doors sway.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: from the very first frame the sleigh is flying, the lighthouse beam is sweeping and six thick white smoke columns are rising from the house chimneys on both walls.
1-2s: the sleigh travels a quarter of the ceiling; a lit ship sails across the harbour behind the bed; a firework bursts; the smoke keeps climbing.
2-3s: the sleigh flies on; a shooting star streaks over; the lights chase along the garlands; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying; the boats rock; the families pull their sledges; two fireworks burst; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
4-5s: everything still moving at full pace in the last frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real adobe plaster, the vigas and latillas, the nicho and the carved pine window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, the bed or the boy.

CAMERA - ONE slow, gentle, continuous tilt DOWN from the first frame to the last, like a parent filming - the camera is NEVER static and the movement is clearly visible in every second.
0.0s: the clip starts EXACTLY on the start image framing - never higher, never wider, never showing anything above the top edge of the start image.
0.0-5.0s: the phone tilts DOWN smoothly at an even pace by about 12 degrees in total: the projected ceiling slides up and partly out of the top of the frame while more of the bed, the covers, the bench and the rug come into view at the bottom. Because the camera only ever moves DOWN from the start framing, no ceiling area outside the start image is ever seen. The boy stays fully in frame from head to feet.
One continuous take: no cut, no zoom, no push-in, no pull-back, never a tilt up, never a locked-off static frame.

THE BOY BOUNCES LIKE A REAL CHILD ON A REAL MATTRESS - natural, believable and unexaggerated, at real speed for all five seconds. Small, springy, continuous bounces on the same spot in the middle of the bed: his feet leave the mattress by only about 10 to 20 centimetres, about two bounces per second, the soft mattress visibly sinking under his feet on every landing and pushing him back up. REAL-TIME SPEED, NOT SLOW MOTION: each bounce is quick and snappy, taking only about half a second from push-off to landing under normal gravity, exactly like a real child filmed at normal speed - the footage is never slowed down, never time-stretched and never dreamy. Knees soft, head tipped back, mouth open, laughing up at the ceiling in amazement. While he bounces he throws one arm straight up and points at Santa's sleigh, turning his head to follow it as it flies across the ceiling, then drops the arm and points again. His body keeps exactly the same size, proportions, face and clothing in every frame; he stays in the same spot on the bed. No high leaps, no hanging in the air, no floating, no hopping across the bed, no stiff puppet-like body, no morphing limbs.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, a child at home with his family at Christmas. Crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed, sharp from first frame to last.

ASSET ROLES: the start image is the approved still that controls a bright-proportioned Santa Fe pueblo-revival bedroom with a high SHED CEILING of dark round vigas and pale latillas sloping up from the front of the room to the tall back wall. A carved knotty-pine bed with a wide upholstered cream headboard is centred on the flat rounded white adobe back wall between two tall deep-set windows with carved pine frames and muntins, a carved pine nicho shelf above, white bedding with a red striped Chimayo wool blanket, pine bedside chests, a saltillo terracotta tile floor and a soft cream wool rug. Preserve the exact child identity, clothing, furniture, colours and the full projected artwork of the start image; its framing is the FIRST frame of this clip.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about six with fair skin and short curly chestnut-brown hair, fully dressed for winter in a long-sleeved forest-green and white brushed-cotton Christmas top printed with little white snowmen and red mittens, with matching loose lounge trousers and white wool socks, standing on the bed. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the walls and the whole sloping viga ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster and the wood is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY CHRISTMAS HARBOUR TOWN: colourful gabled harbour houses with glowing windows and snowy roofs, a curl of white smoke rising from every chimney, little fishing boats strung with Christmas lights bobbing at the quay, a lighthouse sweeping its golden beam across the bay, families pulling sledges along the waterfront, and tall snow-laden fir trees on the hills behind. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the rooftops into a continuous Christmas panorama, with small flat drawn snowflakes sliding down the painted sky. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and small flat drawn snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and harbour blue dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing and gasping in delight. (no BGM). No subtitles.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a still, frozen or printed-looking projection; a projection that only twinkles; a sleigh parked in one place; chimneys without smoke; a house without rising smoke; boats that do not bob; a lighthouse beam that does not sweep; families that do not travel; drawn snow on the walls that does not move; any snowflake, sparkle or particle in the air of the room, in front of the boy or in front of the camera, or anything that looks like real snow falling inside the room; bare, white or unprojected ceiling or wall; a camera that starts higher, wider or anywhere other than the start image; a static, locked-off camera; a tilt up; a pan, zoom, push-in or pull-back; any drawn animal or figure that looks real or leaves the wall; bare or unprojected ceiling in the first frame; any house or cottage without a thick rising smoke column; fewer than twelve things moving at once; a calm or slow projection; slow-motion or floaty jumping; high exaggerated leaps; the boy changing size or shape; real snow or particles in the air of the room; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

Her limbs are heavy and human, not floaty or rubbery, and she never hangs in the air. The ladder goes over with her and clatters onto the rug.

ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and harbour blue dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. The sloping viga ceiling remains clearly three-dimensional, meeting the walls at crisp lines. The sleigh and the ceiling picture are foreshortened along the slope, wrap straight across the round faces of the vigas and the latillas, and fold at the wall line before continuing down the walls. The flat adobe wall, the carved pine window frames and muntins, the nicho and the cream headboard carry properly foreshortened portions of the same continuous projection across their faces. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the harbour town, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert.
